    COUNT(transactions_made) >= 5
).CALCULATE(
    customer_name=name,

).ORDER_BY(success_rate.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q4] Running models for: How many transactions for stocks occurred in each of the last 8 weeks excluding the current week? How many of these transactions happened on weekends?Weekend days are Saturday and Sunday.
[DEBUG] [Q4] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q4] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q4] Code generated by claude:
# Filter transactions for the last 8 weeks excluding current week
transactions_last_8_weeks = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now")) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME("now")) <= 8)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_id=transaction_id,
    week_start=DATETIME(date_time, "start of week"),
    is_weekend=(DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 1) | (DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 7)  # Saturday=7, Sunday=1
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = transactions_last_8_weeks.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start=week_start,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())
DATEDIFF unsupported for 'DAYS'.
DATEDIFF unsupported for 'DAYS'.
[DEBUG] [Q4] DataFrame from claude is valid
[DEBUG] [Q4] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Define the time boundaries for the last 8 weeks, excluding the current week.
# Get the start of the current week. The 'start of week' modifier truncates the date to the beginning of the current week.
current_week_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')

# Calculate the start of the desired period: 8 weeks before the current week's start.
eight_weeks_ago_start = DATETIME(current_week_start, '-8 weeks')

# Calculate the end of the desired period: the day before the current week's start.
# This ensures the current week's transactions are excluded.
end_of_period = DATETIME(current_week_start, '-1 day')

# Step 2: Filter transactions to include only those within the defined 8-week period.
# For each filtered transaction, calculate its week's start date and determine if it occurred on a weekend.
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (Transactions.date_time >= eight_weeks_ago_ago_start) &
    (Transactions.date_time <= end_of_period)
).CALCULATE(
    # 'transaction_week_start' will be used as the partition key to group transactions by week.
    transaction_week_start=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    # 'is_weekend_transaction' is a flag (1 for weekend, 0 for weekday) to easily sum weekend transactions.
    is_weekend_transaction=IFF(
        (DAYNAME(date_time) == "Saturday") | (DAYNAME(date_time) == "Sunday"),
        1,  # Assign 1 if it's a Saturday or Sunday
        0   # Assign 0 otherwise
    )
)

# Step 3: Partition the filtered transactions by their calculated week start date.
# Then, for each week, calculate the total number of transactions and the number of weekend transactions.
weekly_transaction_summary = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups",  # Name the partitioned group 'weekly_groups'
    by=transaction_week_start  # Group by the start date of the week
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=transaction_week_start,  # Include the week start date in the final output
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),  # Count all transactions within each weekly group
    weekend_transactions=SUM(is_weekend_transaction)  # Sum the 'is_weekend_transaction' flags to count weekend transactions
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())  # Order the results by week start date in ascending order

[DEBUG] [Q5] Running models for: How many transactions were made by customers from the USA last week (exclusive of the current week)? Return the number of transactions and total transaction amount.
[DEBUG] [Q5] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q5] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q5] Code generated by gemini:
# Calculate the start and end dates for "last week" (exclusive of the current week)
# at the graph level to make them available for filtering.
transaction_period_info = Broker.CALCULATE(
    start_of_current_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    start_of_last_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '- 7 days')
)

# Filter customers to only include those from the USA.
usa_customers_transactions = transaction_period_info.Customers.WHERE(
    country == "USA"
).transactions_made.WHERE(
    # Filter transactions to be within "last week"
    # (from the start of last week up to, but not including, the start of the current week).
    (date_time >= start_of_last_week) & (date_time < start_of_current_week)
)

# Calculate the total number of transactions and the sum of their amounts.
result = usa_customers_transactions.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions=COUNT(transaction_id),
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(amount)
)
[DEBUG] [Q5] DataFrame from gemini is None
[DEBUG] [Q5] Code generated by claude:
# Get transactions from USA customers made last week (exclusive of current week)
usa_transactions_last_week = Customers.WHERE(country == "USA").transactions_made.WHERE(
    # Last week: 7-14 days ago (exclusive of current week)
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) >= 7) &
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) < 14)
)

# Calculate the results
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions=COUNT(usa_transactions_last_week),
    total_amount=SUM(usa_transactions_last_week.amount)
)

# Calculate the start and end dates for "last week" (exclusive of the current week)
# at the graph level to make them available for filtering.
transaction_period_info = Broker.CALCULATE(
    start_of_current_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    start_of_last_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '- 7 days')
)

# Filter customers to only include those from the USA.
usa_customers_transactions = transaction_period_info.Customers.WHERE(
    country == "USA"
).transactions_made.WHERE(
    # Filter transactions to be within "last week"
    # (from the start of last week up to, but not including, the start of the current week).
    (date_time >= start_of_last_week) & (date_time < start_of_current_week)
)

# Calculate the total number of transactions and the sum of their amounts.
result = usa_customers_transactions.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions=COUNT(transaction_id),

# Step 1: Calculate the date 10 days ago from the current timestamp
ten_days_ago = DATETIME('now', '-10 days')

# Step 2: Filter transactions for 'BUY' type and within the last 10 days
recent_buy_transactions = Broker.Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == 'BUY') &
    (date_time >= ten_days_ago)
)

# Step 3: Prepare data for partitioning by calculating the ticker symbol
# This flattens the nested 'ticker.symbol' into a direct field 'ticker_symbol'
transactions_with_symbol = recent_buy_transactions.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol
)

# Step 4: Group by ticker symbol and count the number of buy transactions for each symbol
ticker_buy_counts = transactions_with_symbol.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups", by=ticker_symbol
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    num_buy_transactions=COUNT(transactions_with_symbol)
)

# Step 5: Select the top 2 most frequently bought ticker symbols
result = ticker_buy_counts.TOP_K(
    2, by=num_buy_transactions.DESC()
)

[DEBUG] [Q8] Running models for: What are the PMCS and PMAT for customers who signed up in the last 6 months excluding the current month?PMCS = per month customer signups. PMAT = per month average transaction amount.

[DEBUG] [Q8] Code generated by gemini:
# Calculate the start of the current month and the start of the month 6 months prior
current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
six_months_ago_start = DATETIME(current_month_start, '-6 months')

# Filter customers who signed up in the last 6 months, excluding the current month
# and extract their signup year and month.
customer_signup_data = Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= six_months_ago_start) & (join_date < current_month_start)
).CALCULATE(
    signup_year=YEAR(join_date),
    signup_month=MONTH(join_date)
)

# Partition the filtered customer data by signup year and month.
# Then, for each partition (each signup month), calculate:
# 1. PMCS (per month customer signups): Count of customers in that month.
# 2. PMAT (per month average transaction amount): Average of all transaction amounts
#    made by customers who signed up in that specific month.
result = customer_signup_data.PARTITION(
    name="signup_groups", by=(signup_year, signup_month)
).CALCULATE(
    signup_year=signup_year,
    signup_month=signup_month,
    pmcs=COUNT(Customers),  # Count of customers who signed up in this month
    pmat=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)  # Average transaction amount for these customers
).ORDER_BY(signup_year.ASC(), signup_month.ASC()) # Order results chronologically
ERROR WHILE EXECUTING QUERY:
WITH _s2 AS (
  SELECT
    COUNT() AS agg_1,
    CAST(STRFTIME('%m', sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER) AS signup_month,
    CAST(STRFTIME('%Y', sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER) AS signup_year
  FROM main.sbcustomer
  WHERE
    sbcustjoindate < DATE('now', 'start of month')
    AND sbcustjoindate >= DATETIME(DATE('now', 'start of month'), '-6 month')
  GROUP BY
    CAST(STRFTIME('%m', sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER),
    CAST(STRFTIME('%Y', sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER)
), _s1 AS (
  SELECT
    SUM(sbtxamount) AS expr_0,
    COUNT(sbtxamount) AS expr_1,
    sbtxcustid AS customer_id
  FROM main.sbtransaction
  GROUP BY
    sbtxcustid
), _t4 AS (
  SELECT
    SUM(_s1.expr_0) AS expr_0,
    SUM(_s1.expr_1) AS expr_1,
    CAST(STRFTIME('%m', sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER) AS signup_month,
    CAST(STRFTIME('%Y', sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER) AS signup_year
  FROM main.sbcustomer AS sbcustomer
  JOIN _s1 AS _s1
    ON _s1.customer_id = sbcustomer.sbcustid
  WHERE
    sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate < DATE('now', 'start of month')
    AND sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate >= DATETIME(DATE('now', 'start of month'), '-6 month')
  GROUP BY
    CAST(STRFTIME('%m', sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER),
    CAST(STRFTIME('%Y', sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER)
)
SELECT
  _s2.signup_year,
  _s2.signup_month,
  COALESCE(_s2.agg_1, 0) AS pmcs,
  CAST(_t4.expr_0 AS REAL) / _t4.expr_1 AS pmat
FROM _s2 AS _s2
LEFT JOIN _t4 AS _t4
  ON _s2.signup_month = _t4.signup_month AND _s2.signup_year = _t4.signup_year
ORDER BY
  signup_year,
  signup_month
[DEBUG] [Q8] DataFrame from gemini is None
[DEBUG] [Q8] Code generated by claude:
# Define the date range: last 6 months excluding current month
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    MONOTONIC(
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months'),
        join_date,
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 day')
    )
)

# Calculate customer info with join month
customer_info = recent_customers.CALCULATE(
    customer_id=_id,
    join_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Group by month to calculate PMCS (per month customer signups)
pmcs_data = customer_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=join_month).CALCULATE(
    join_month=join_month,
    pmcs=COUNT(Customers)
)

# Calculate PMAT (per month average transaction amount) for these customers
# First get transaction info for these customers in the same time period
transaction_info = recent_customers.transactions_made.WHERE(
    MONOTONIC(
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months'),
        date_time,
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 day')
    )
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date_time), LPAD(MONTH(date_time), 2, "0")),
    amount=amount
)

# Group transactions by month to calculate PMAT
pmat_data = transaction_info.PARTITION(name="tx_months", by=transaction_month).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month=transaction_month,
    pmat=AVG(Transactions.amount)
)

# Combine PMCS and PMAT data
result = pmcs_data.CALCULATE(
    month=join_month,
    pmcs=pmcs,
    pmat=DEFAULT_TO(pmat_data.WHERE(transaction_month == join_month).SINGULAR().pmat, 0)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Define the date range: last 6 months excluding current month
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    MONOTONIC(
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months'),
        join_date,
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 day')
    )
)

# Calculate customer info with month of signup
customer_info = recent_customers.CALCULATE(
    customer_id=_id,
    signup_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Group by signup month to calculate PMCS
pmcs_data = customer_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=signup_month).CALCULATE(
    signup_month=signup_month,
    pmcs=COUNT(Customers)
)

# Calculate PMAT for each month - average transaction amount per customer per month
customer_transactions = customer_info.CALCULATE(
    signup_month=signup_month,
    transaction_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(transactions_made.date_time), LPAD(MONTH(transactions_made.date_time), 2, "0")),
    transaction_amount=transactions_made.amount
)

# Group by signup month and transaction month to get average transaction amounts
pmat_data = customer_transactions.PARTITION(name="signup_months", by=signup_month).PARTITION(name="transaction_months", by=transaction_month).CALCULATE(
    signup_month=signup_month,
    transaction_month=transaction_month,
    pmat=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
)

# Combine the results
result = pmcs_data.CALCULATE(
    signup_month=signup_month,
    pmcs=pmcs,
    pmat=AVG(pmat_data.WHERE(signup_month == signup_month).pmat)
).ORDER_BY(signup_month.ASC())

# Calculate the start of the current month and the start of the month 6 months prior

six_months_ago_start = DATETIME(current_month_start, '-6 months')

# Filter customers who signed up in the last 6 months, excluding the current month
# and extract their signup year and month.
customer_signup_data = Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= six_months_ago_start) & (join_date < current_month_start)
).CALCULATE(
    signup_year=YEAR(join_date),
    signup_month=MONTH(join_date)
)

# Partition the filtered customer data by signup year and month.
# Then, for each partition (each signup month), calculate:
# 1. PMCS (per month customer signups): Count of customers in that month.
# 2. PMAT (per month average transaction amount): Average of all transaction amounts
#    made by customers who signed up in that specific month.
result = customer_signup_data.PARTITION(
    name="signup_groups", by=(signup_year, signup_month)
).CALCULATE(
    signup_year=signup_year,
    signup_month=signup_month,
    pmcs=COUNT(Customers),  # Count of customers who signed up in this month
    pmat=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)  # Average transaction amount for these customers
).ORDER_BY(signup_year.ASC(), signup_month.ASC()) # Order results chronologically
ERROR WHILE EXECUTING QUERY:
WITH _s2 AS (
  SELECT
    COUNT() AS agg_1,
    CAST(STRFTIME('%m', sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER) AS signup_month,
    CAST(STRFTIME('%Y', sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER) AS signup_year
  FROM main.sbcustomer
  WHERE
    sbcustjoindate < DATE('now', 'start of month')
    AND sbcustjoindate >= DATETIME(DATE('now', 'start of month'), '-6 month')
  GROUP BY
    CAST(STRFTIME('%m', sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER),
    CAST(STRFTIME('%Y', sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER)
), _s1 AS (
  SELECT
    SUM(sbtxamount) AS expr_0,
    COUNT(sbtxamount) AS expr_1,
    sbtxcustid AS customer_id
  FROM main.sbtransaction
  GROUP BY
    sbtxcustid
), _t4 AS (
  SELECT
    SUM(_s1.expr_0) AS expr_0,
    SUM(_s1.expr_1) AS expr_1,
    CAST(STRFTIME('%m', sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER) AS signup_month,
    CAST(STRFTIME('%Y', sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER) AS signup_year
  FROM main.sbcustomer AS sbcustomer
  JOIN _s1 AS _s1
    ON _s1.customer_id = sbcustomer.sbcustid
  WHERE
    sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate < DATE('now', 'start of month')
    AND sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate >= DATETIME(DATE('now', 'start of month'), '-6 month')
  GROUP BY
    CAST(STRFTIME('%m', sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER),
    CAST(STRFTIME('%Y', sbcustomer.sbcustjoindate) AS INTEGER)
)
SELECT
  _s2.signup_year,
  _s2.signup_month,
  COALESCE(_s2.agg_1, 0) AS pmcs,
  CAST(_t4.expr_0 AS REAL) / _t4.expr_1 AS pmat
FROM _s2 AS _s2
LEFT JOIN _t4 AS _t4
  ON _s2.signup_month = _t4.signup_month AND _s2.signup_year = _t4.signup_year
ORDER BY
  signup_year,
  signup_month

[DEBUG] [Q10] Running models for: What is the AR for each country for customers who joined in 2022? Return the country and AR.AR (Activity Ratio) = (Number of Active Customers with Transactions / Total Number of Customers with Transactions) * 100.
[DEBUG] [Q10] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q10] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q10] Code generated by claude:
# Filter customers who joined in 2022 and have transactions
customers_2022_with_transactions = Customers.WHERE(
    (YEAR(join_date) == 2022) & (HAS(transactions_made) == 1)
)

# Group by country and calculate AR
result = customers_2022_with_transactions.PARTITION(name="countries", by=country).CALCULATE(
    country=country,
    total_customers_with_transactions=COUNT(Customers),
    active_customers_with_transactions=COUNT(Customers.WHERE(status == "Active")),
    AR=100.0 * COUNT(Customers.WHERE(status == "Active")) / COUNT(Customers)
)
[DEBUG] [Q10] DataFrame from claude is valid
[DEBUG] [Q10] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter customers who joined in 2022
customers_joined_2022 = Customers.WHERE(YEAR(join_date) == 2022)

# Step 2 & 3: Partition by country and calculate the necessary counts
country_summary = customers_joined_2022.PARTITION(name="country_group", by=country).CALCULATE(
    country_name=country, # Use a temporary name for the country field
    total_customers_with_transactions=COUNT(country_group.WHERE(HAS(transactions_made) == 1)),
    active_customers_with_transactions=COUNT(country_group.WHERE((status == 'Active') & HAS(transactions_made) == 1))
)

# Step 4 & 5: Calculate the Activity Ratio (AR) and select the final fields
result = country_summary.CALCULATE(
    country=country_name,
    AR=IFF(
        total_customers_with_transactions > 0,
        (active_customers_with_transactions / total_customers_with_transactions) * 100.0,
        0.0 # Return 0.0 if there are no customers with transactions to avoid division by zero
    )
)

# Filter sell transactions from the past month
sell_transactions_past_month = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "sell") &
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30)
)

# Group by ticker symbol and calculate SPM
result = sell_transactions_past_month.PARTITION(name="ticker_groups", by=ticker_id).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    total_amount=SUM(Transactions.amount),
    total_costs=SUM(Transactions.tax) + SUM(Transactions.commission),
    spm=((SUM(Transactions.amount) - (SUM(Transactions.tax) + SUM(Transactions.commission))) / SUM(Transactions.amount)) * 100
)

# Step 1: Filter for sell transactions within the last month
recent_sell_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (Transactions.transaction_type == "sell") &
    (Transactions.date_time >= DATETIME('now', '-1 month'))
)

# Step 2 & 3: Partition by ticker symbol and aggregate the necessary values
ticker_spm_calculations = recent_sell_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups",
    by=recent_sell_transactions.ticker.symbol
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_name=recent_sell_transactions.ticker.symbol,
    total_sell_amount=SUM(recent_sell_transactions.amount),
    total_sell_tax=SUM(recent_sell_transactions.tax),
    total_sell_commission=SUM(recent_sell_transactions.commission)
)

# Step 4 & 5: Calculate SPM and select the final results
result = ticker_spm_calculations.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol_name,
    spm=IFF(
        total_sell_amount == 0,
        0.0,  # Handle division by zero by returning 0.0 if total_sell_amount is zero
        ((total_sell_amount - (total_sell_tax + total_sell_commission)) / total_sell_amount) * 100
    )
)

# Step 1: Extract month and ticker symbol from daily prices
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    month = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0")),
    symbol = ticker.symbol
)

# Step 2: Partition by symbol and month to calculate monthly statistics
monthly_stats = price_info.PARTITION(name="ticker_months", by=(symbol, month)).CALCULATE(
    symbol = symbol,
    month = month,
    avg_close = AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    highest_price = MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    lowest_price = MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Calculate month-over-month change using PREV function
result = monthly_stats.CALCULATE(
    symbol = symbol,
    month = month,
    avg_close = avg_close,
    highest_price = highest_price,
    lowest_price = lowest_price,
    MoMC = (avg_close - PREV(avg_close, by=(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0)) / PREV(avg_close, by=(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=1)
).ORDER_BY(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q13] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Prepare DailyPrices by extracting the ticker symbol, formatting the month,
# and creating a date object for proper chronological ordering.
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    symbol=ticker.symbol,
    month_str=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0")),
    month_date=DATETIME(date, 'start of month')
)

# Step 2: Partition the data by ticker symbol and formatted month to calculate
# the average closing price, highest price, and lowest price for each month.
monthly_stats = price_info.PARTITION(name="ticker_months", by=(symbol, month_str)).CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month_str=month_str,
    avg_close=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    max_high=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low=MIN(DailyPrices.low),
    month_date=MIN(DailyPrices.month_date) # Keep the month_date for ordering in the next step
)

# Step 3: Re-partition the monthly statistics by ticker symbol. This creates groups
# where we can apply window functions (like PREV) per ticker.
ticker_symbol_groups = monthly_stats.PARTITION(name="ticker_symbol_groups", by=symbol)

# Step 4: Calculate the Month-over-Month Change (MoMC) using the PREV function.
# PREV looks at the previous month's average closing price within each ticker symbol group.
result = ticker_symbol_groups.ticker_months.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month_str,
    avg_close=avg_close,
    max_high=max_high,
    min_low=min_low,
    # Calculate the previous month's average closing price for the same ticker
    prev_avg_close=PREV(avg_close, by=month_date.ASC(), per="ticker_symbol_groups"),
    # Calculate MoMC, handling division by zero
    momc=IFF(PREV(avg_close, by=month_date.ASC(), per="ticker_symbol_groups") != 0,
             (avg_close - PREV(avg_close, by=month_date.ASC(), per="ticker_symbol_groups")) / PREV(avg_close, by=month_date.ASC(), per="ticker_symbol_groups"),
             None)
)

# Step 1: Prepare price data with month and ticker symbol
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(

[DEBUG] [Q13] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Prepare DailyPrices data by extracting the ticker symbol and formatting the date to month
daily_price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    symbol=ticker.symbol,
    formatted_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0")),
    close_price=close,
    high_price=high,
    low_price=low
)

# Step 2: Group the daily price information by ticker symbol and formatted month
# Calculate the average closing price, highest price, and lowest price for each month
monthly_ticker_data = daily_price_info.PARTITION(name="ticker_month_groups", by=(symbol, formatted_month)).CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=formatted_month,
    avg_close=AVG(daily_price_info.close_price),
    max_high=MAX(daily_price_info.high_price),
    min_low=MIN(daily_price_info.low_price)
)

# Step 3: Partition the monthly aggregated data again by ticker symbol
# This creates a context ('ticker_groups') that allows us to use the PREV function
# to look at the previous month's data *within* each ticker's history.
final_data_with_prev = monthly_ticker_data.PARTITION(name="ticker_groups", by=symbol).ticker_month_groups.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=avg_close,
    max_high=max_high,
    min_low=min_low,
    # Calculate the previous month's average close for the same ticker, ordered by month
    prev_avg_close=PREV(avg_close, by=month.ASC(), per="ticker_groups")
)

# Step 4: Calculate the Month-over-Month Change (MoMC)
# Handle cases where prev_avg_close might be None (for the first month of a ticker) or zero
result = final_data_with_prev.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=avg_close,
    max_high=max_high,
    min_low=min_low,
    momc=IFF(
        DEFAULT_TO(prev_avg_close, 0) != 0, # Check if previous average close is not zero or null
        (avg_close - prev_avg_close) / prev_avg_close,
        None # Set MoMC to None if prev_avg_close is None or 0
    )
).ORDER_BY(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC()) # Order the results for better readability

# Step 1: Filter DailyPrices for the specified date range and include the ticker symbol
filtered_daily_prices = DailyPrices.WHERE(
    (date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04")
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    low_price=low,
    high_price=high
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered prices by ticker symbol to find the min low and max high for each stock
ticker_price_ranges = filtered_daily_prices.PARTITION(
    name="stock_prices", by=ticker_symbol
).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    min_low_for_range=MIN(stock_prices.low_price),
    max_high_for_range=MAX(stock_prices.high_price)
)

# Step 3: Calculate the price change for each stock
price_changes = ticker_price_ranges.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    price_change=max_high_for_range - min_low_for_range
)

# Step 4: Select the top 3 distinct stocks with the highest price change
result = price_changes.TOP_K(3, by=price_change.DESC())

    (date >= "2023-04-01") & (date <= "2023-04-04")
)

# Group by ticker and calculate price change for each stock

    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    max_high=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low=MIN(DailyPrices.low),
    price_change=MAX(DailyPrices.high) - MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Get the top 3 stocks with highest price change
result = stock_price_changes.TOP_K(3, by=price_change.DESC()).CALCULATE(

)

# First, calculate the join year and month for each customer.
customer_join_info = Customers.CALCULATE(
    customer_id_alias=_id,
    customer_name_alias=name,
    join_year_val=YEAR(join_date),
    join_month_val=MONTH(join_date)
)

# Then, for each customer, count the transactions that occurred in their join month and year.
# Finally, select the top customer based on this count.
highest_transactions_customer = customer_join_info.CALCULATE(
    _id=customer_id_alias,
    name=customer_name_alias,
    num_transactions=COUNT(
        transactions_made.WHERE(
            (YEAR(date_time) == join_year_val) & (MONTH(date_time) == join_month_val)
        )
    )
).TOP_K(1, by=num_transactions.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q15] Code generated by claude:
# Calculate join month/year for each customer and count transactions in that same month
customer_join_month_transactions = Customers.CALCULATE(
    customer_id=_id,
    customer_name=name,
    join_year=YEAR(join_date),
    join_month=MONTH(join_date),
    transactions_in_join_month=COUNT(
        transactions_made.WHERE(
            (YEAR(date_time) == YEAR(join_date)) & 
            (MONTH(date_time) == MONTH(join_date))
        )
    )
).WHERE(transactions_in_join_month > 0)

# Find the customer with the highest number of transactions in their join month
result = customer_join_month_transactions.TOP_K(1, by=transactions_in_join_month.DESC()).CALCULATE(
    customer_id=customer_id,
    customer_name=customer_name,
    num_transactions=transactions_in_join_month
)

[DEBUG] [Q17] Running models for: How many distinct customers made each type of transaction between Jan 1, 2023 and Mar 31, 2023 (inclusive of start and end dates)? Return the transaction type, number of distinct customers and average number of shares, for the top 3 transaction types by number of customers.
[DEBUG] [Q17] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q17] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q17] Code generated by gemini:
# Filter transactions within the specified date range (Jan 1, 2023 to Mar 31, 2023)
transactions_in_range = Broker.Transactions.WHERE(
    (date_time >= "2023-01-01") & (date_time <= "2023-03-31")
)

# Partition the filtered transactions by transaction type
# Then, for each transaction type, calculate the number of distinct customers and the average shares
transaction_type_stats = transactions_in_range.PARTITION(
    name="transaction_type_groups", by=transaction_type
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type=transaction_type,
    num_distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),
    avg_shares=AVG(Transactions.shares)
)

# Select the top 3 transaction types based on the number of distinct customers
# and return the requested fields
result = transaction_type_stats.TOP_K(
    3, by=num_distinct_customers.DESC()
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type,
    num_distinct_customers,
    avg_shares
)
[DEBUG] [Q17] DataFrame from gemini is valid
[DEBUG] [Q17] Code generated by claude:
# Filter transactions to the specified date range (Jan 1, 2023 to Mar 31, 2023)
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (date_time >= "2023-01-01") & (date_time <= "2023-03-31")
)

# Group by transaction type and calculate metrics
transaction_type_stats = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="transaction_types", 
    by=transaction_type
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type=transaction_type,
    distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),
    avg_shares=AVG(Transactions.shares)
)

# Get top 3 transaction types by number of distinct customers
result = transaction_type_stats.TOP_K(3, by=distinct_customers.DESC())

    customer_state=customer.state,
    ticker_type=ticker.ticker_type
)

).CALCULATE(

[DEBUG] [Q26] Running models for: What are the top 5 countries by total transaction amount in the past 30 days, inclusive of 30 days ago? Return the country name, number of transactions and total transaction amount.
[DEBUG] [Q26] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q26] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q26] Code generated by gemini:
# Calculate the date 30 days ago from the current timestamp
thirty_days_ago = DATETIME('now', '-30 days')

# Filter transactions that occurred within the last 30 days (inclusive)
# and calculate the country name and transaction amount for each
recent_transactions_info = Transactions.WHERE(
    date_time >= thirty_days_ago
).CALCULATE(
    country_name=customer.country,
    transaction_amount=amount
)

# Partition the filtered transactions by country and calculate the total amount and count
country_transaction_summary = recent_transactions_info.PARTITION(
    name="countries", by=country_name
).CALCULATE(
    country_name=country_name,
    num_of_transactions=COUNT(recent_transactions_info),
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(recent_transactions_info.transaction_amount)
)

# Select the top 5 countries based on their total transaction amount
result = country_transaction_summary.TOP_K(5, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC())
[DEBUG] [Q26] DataFrame from gemini is valid
[DEBUG] [Q26] Code generated by claude:
# Filter transactions from the past 30 days and add country information
recent_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30
).CALCULATE(
    country=customer.country,
    transaction_amount=amount
)

# Group by country and calculate aggregations
country_stats = recent_transactions.PARTITION(name="countries", by=country).CALCULATE(
    country_name=country,
    num_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(Transactions.amount)
)

# Get top 5 countries by total transaction amount
result = country_stats.TOP_K(5, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC())

Return the week (as a date), total payments received, and weekend payments received in ascending order.

# Get the date range for last 8 weeks (excluding current week)
date_info = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-0 weeks'),
    eight_weeks_ago=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-9 weeks')
)

# Get payments from high-value sales in the target date range
high_value_payments = date_info.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= eight_weeks_ago) & 
    (payment_date < current_week_start)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (1, 7))
)

# Group by week and calculate totals
result = high_value_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_date=week_start,
    total_payments=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_date.ASC())
[DEBUG] [Q32] DataFrame from claude is None
[DEBUG] [Q32] Code generated by gemini:
# 1. Filter sales with a sale price over $30,000 and access their associated payments received.
# 2. For each payment, calculate the start of its calendar week and determine if it occurred on a weekend.
payments_info = Dealership.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.CALCULATE(
    payment_week=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(payment_date), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# 3. Define the date range for the last 8 calendar weeks, excluding the current week.
#    'current_week_start_date' marks the beginning of the current week.
#    'eight_weeks_ago_start_date' marks the beginning of the week 8 weeks prior to the current week.
#    We filter payments that fall within this range: [eight_weeks_ago_start_date, current_week_start_date).
current_week_start_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
eight_weeks_ago_start_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '- 8 weeks')

filtered_payments = payments_info.WHERE(
    (payment_week >= eight_weeks_ago_start_date) &
    (payment_week < current_week_start_date)
)

# 4. Partition the filtered payments by their calculated week.
# 5. For each week, calculate the total number of payments received and the number of payments received on weekends.
#    SUM(KEEP_IF(1, is_weekend)) effectively counts payments where 'is_weekend' is True.
result = filtered_payments.PARTITION(name="weekly_groups", by=payment_week).CALCULATE(
    week_date=payment_week,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(KEEP_IF(1, is_weekend))
).ORDER_BY(week_date.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q32] Running model gemini (attempt 1)
[DEBUG] [Q32] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Calculate the start of the current week and the start of the 8th week prior to the current week.
# These values are calculated at the top-level graph to be accessible for filtering later.
graph_context = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    eight_weeks_ago_start = DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-8 weeks')
)

# Step 2: Filter sales with a sale price over $30,000, then access their associated payments.
# For each payment, calculate its week start date and determine if it occurred on a weekend.
# Downstream the calculated week boundaries from the graph_context.
payments_info = graph_context.Sales.WHERE(
    sale_price > 30000
).payment.CALCULATE(
    payment_week_start = DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend = (DAYNAME(payment_date) == "Saturday") | (DAYNAME(payment_date) == "Sunday"),
    # Downstream the week boundary values for filtering in the next step
    current_week_start = current_week_start,
    eight_weeks_ago_start = eight_weeks_ago_start
)

# Step 3: Filter these payments to include only those within the last 8 calendar weeks,
# explicitly excluding the current week.
filtered_payments = payments_info.WHERE(
    (payment_week_start >= eight_weeks_ago_start) &
    (payment_week_start < current_week_start)
)

# Step 4: Partition the filtered payments by their week start date.
# For each week, calculate the total number of payments received and the number of payments received on weekends.
weekly_payments_summary = filtered_payments.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups", by=payment_week_start
).CALCULATE(
    week_date = payment_week_start,
    total_payments_received = COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received = SUM(IFF(is_weekend, 1, 0))
)

# Step 5: Order the final results by the week date in ascending order.
result = weekly_payments_summary.ORDER_BY(week_date.ASC())

Use the most recent date in the inventory_snapshots table to determine that car's inventory status.When getting a car's inventory status, always take the latest status from the inventory_snapshots table

[DEBUG] [Q37] Code generated by claude:
# First, get cars with their most recent inventory status
cars_with_latest_inventory = Cars.WHERE(
    HAS(sale_records) == 1 & HAS(inventory_snapshots) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=make,
    car_model=model,
    latest_inventory_status=inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC()) == 1
    ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory,
    max_sale_price=MAX(sale_records.sale_price)
)

# Filter for cars no longer in inventory and group by make/model
result = cars_with_latest_inventory.WHERE(
    latest_inventory_status == False
).PARTITION(name="make_model_groups", by=(car_make, car_model)).CALCULATE(
    make=car_make,
    model=car_model,
    highest_sale_price=MAX(Cars.max_sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())
[DEBUG] [Q37] DataFrame from claude is None
[DEBUG] [Q37] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Identify cars that are no longer in inventory based on their latest snapshot.
# This involves ranking inventory snapshots by date for each car and filtering for the latest 'is_in_inventory = False' status.
cars_not_in_inventory_ids = InventorySnapshots.CALCULATE(
    # Rank snapshots for each car by snapshot_date in descending order.
    # 'per="car"' ensures the ranking is done independently for each car.
    snapshot_rank=RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), per="car")
).WHERE(
    # Filter to keep only the most recent snapshot for each car
    (snapshot_rank == 1)
    # And ensure that this latest snapshot indicates the car is no longer in inventory
    & (is_in_inventory == False)
).CALCULATE(
    # Select the car_id of these cars. This will be a list of car IDs.
    car_id_not_in_inv=car_id
)

# Step 2: Filter sales records to include only those for cars identified as no longer in inventory.
# Then, prepare the data for grouping by make and model.
sales_info = Sales.WHERE(
    # Check if the car_id of the current sale is present in the list of car_ids that are not in inventory.
    ISIN(car_id, cars_not_in_inventory_ids.car_id_not_in_inv)
).CALCULATE(
    # Extract the make and model directly from the associated 'car' sub-collection.
    car_make=car.make,
    car_model=car.model,
    # Keep the sale price for aggregation.
    sale_price_val=sale_price
)

# Step 3: Group the filtered sales by car make and model, and calculate the highest sale price for each group.
highest_sale_prices_per_make_model = sales_info.PARTITION(
    name="car_groups", by=(car_make, car_model)
).CALCULATE(
    # Select the make and model as grouping keys.
    make=car_make,
    model=car_model,
    # Calculate the maximum sale price within each make-model group.
    highest_sale_price=MAX(sales_info.sale_price_val)
)

# Step 4: Order the final results by the highest sale price in descending order.
result = highest_sale_prices_per_make_model.ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q39] Running models for: What are the PMSPS and PMSR in the last 6 months excluding the current month, for salespersons hired between 2022 and 2023 (both inclusive)? Return all months in your answer, including those where metrics are 0. Order by month ascending.PMSPS = per month salesperson sales count. PMSR = per month sales revenue in dollars. Truncate date to month for aggregation.
[DEBUG] [Q39] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q39] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q39] Code generated by claude:
# Filter salespersons hired between 2022-2023 (inclusive)
eligible_salespersons = Salespersons.WHERE(
    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
)

# Get all sales from eligible salespersons and add month information
sales_with_month = eligible_salespersons.sales_made.CALCULATE(
    sale_month = DATETIME(sale_date, "start of month"),
    sale_price = sale_price
).WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_month, DATETIME("now", "start of month")) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_month, DATETIME("now", "start of month")) <= 6)
)

# Group by month and calculate metrics
result = sales_with_month.PARTITION(name="months", by=sale_month).CALCULATE(
    month = sale_month,
    pmsps = COUNT(sales_made),
    pmsr = SUM(sales_made.sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())
[DEBUG] [Q39] DataFrame from claude is valid
[DEBUG] [Q39] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Define the time window for the last 6 months, excluding the current month
current_date_time = DATETIME('now')
start_of_current_month = DATETIME(current_date_time, 'start of month')
start_of_6_months_ago = DATETIME(start_of_current_month, '-6 months')

# Step 2: Prepare sales data by calculating the sale month and salesperson's hire year
# This initial step includes all sales to ensure all months with any sales are considered for partitioning.
all_sales_info = Dealership.Sales.CALCULATE(
    sale_month_start = DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month'),
    salesperson_hire_year = YEAR(salesperson.hire_date),
    sale_price = sale_price
)

# Step 3: Filter the prepared sales data to include only sales within the specified 6-month window
sales_in_window = all_sales_info.WHERE(
    (sale_month_start >= start_of_6_months_ago) &
    (sale_month_start < start_of_current_month)
)

# Step 4: Partition the sales within the window by month and calculate PMSPS and PMSR.
# We use KEEP_IF to conditionally include sales based on the salesperson's hire year.
# This ensures that if a month has sales but none from eligible salespersons, the metrics will be 0.
result = sales_in_window.PARTITION(name="monthly_sales_group", by=sale_month_start).CALCULATE(
    month = sale_month_start,
    # PMSPS: Count sales where the salesperson was hired between 2022 and 2023
    PMSPS = COUNT(KEEP_IF(sales_in_window.sale_price, (sales_in_window.salesperson_hire_year >= 2022) & (sales_in_window.salesperson_hire_year <= 2023))),
    # PMSR: Sum sale_price where the salesperson was hired between 2022 and 2023
    PMSR = SUM(KEEP_IF(sales_in_window.sale_price, (sales_in_window.salesperson_hire_year >= 2022) & (sales_in_window.salesperson_hire_year <= 2023)))
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q43] Running model claude (attempt 0)

# Find the last inventory date for each car where it was still in inventory
cars_with_last_inventory_date = Cars.WHERE(
    HAS(inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == True)) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=make,
    car_model=model,
    last_inventory_date=MAX(inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == True).snapshot_date)
)

# Find sales that occurred on the same day the car went out of inventory
same_day_sales = cars_with_last_inventory_date.sale_records.WHERE(
    sale_date == last_inventory_date
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=car_make,
    car_model=car_model,
    sale_price=sale_price
)

# Find the sale with the highest price
result = same_day_sales.TOP_K(1, by=sale_price.DESC()).CALCULATE(
    make=car_make,
    model=car_model,
    sale_price=sale_price
)
[DEBUG] [Q43] DataFrame from claude is valid
[DEBUG] [Q43] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter Sales records where the sale_date matches the date the car went out of inventory.
# We access the 'car' sub-collection from 'Sales', then its 'inventory_snapshots'.
# We filter these snapshots for 'is_in_inventory == False' to find when it left inventory.
# We use SINGULAR() assuming there's one relevant snapshot for a car's departure from inventory due to a sale.
sold_on_inventory_exit_day = Sales.WHERE(
    sale_date == car.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == False).SINGULAR().snapshot_date
)

# Step 2: From these filtered sales, find the car with the highest sale price.
# TOP_K(1, by=sale_price.DESC()) selects the single record with the highest sale_price.
highest_price_car_on_exit_day = sold_on_inventory_exit_day.TOP_K(1, by=sale_price.DESC())

# Step 3: Select the make, model, and sale price of this car.
# 'car.make' and 'car.model' access attributes from the related 'Cars' collection.
result = highest_price_car_on_exit_day.CALCULATE(
    make=car.make,
    model=car.model,
    sale_price=sale_price
)

[DEBUG] [Q43] Code generated by claude:
# Find cars that were sold on the same day they went out of inventory
cars_sold_same_day = Cars.WHERE(
    HAS(sale_records) == 1 & HAS(inventory_snapshots) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=make,
    car_model=model,
    # Find the last inventory date when the car was in inventory
    last_inventory_date=MAX(inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == True).snapshot_date),
    # Get sale information for sales that match the last inventory date
    matching_sales=sale_records.WHERE(sale_date == MAX(inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == True).snapshot_date))
).WHERE(
    HAS(matching_sales) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=car_make,
    car_model=car_model,
    highest_sale_price=MAX(matching_sales.sale_price)
)

# Find the car with the highest sale price among those sold on inventory exit day
result = cars_sold_same_day.TOP_K(1, by=highest_sale_price.DESC()).CALCULATE(
    make=car_make,
    model=car_model,
    sale_price=highest_sale_price
)

[DEBUG] [Q44] Code generated by claude:
# Step 1: Extract month/year info and group payments by month
payment_info = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    year=YEAR(payment_date),
    month=MONTH(payment_date),
    year_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(payment_date), LPAD(MONTH(payment_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Step 2: Group by year-month and calculate total payments per month
monthly_payments = payment_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=(year, month)).CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    month=month,
    year_month=year_month,
    total_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoM change using PREV function
result = monthly_payments.CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    month=month,
    year_month=year_month,
    total_payments=total_payments,
    prev_month_payments=PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0),
    mom_change=total_payments - PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0)
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC(), month.ASC())

# Step 1: Extract year and month from payment_date
payments_info = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    payment_year=YEAR(payment_date),
    payment_month=MONTH(payment_date)
)

# Step 2: Partition by year and month to sum payments for each month
monthly_payments_summary = payments_info.PARTITION(name="monthly_data", by=(payment_year, payment_month)).CALCULATE(
    payment_year=payment_year,
    payment_month=payment_month,
    total_payments_for_month=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoM change using PREV and format the month_year
result = monthly_payments_summary.CALCULATE(
    month_year=JOIN_STRINGS('-', payment_year, LPAD(payment_month, 2, '0')),
    total_payments=total_payments_for_month,
    mom_change=total_payments_for_month - PREV(total_payments_for_month, by=(payment_year.ASC(), payment_month.ASC()))
).ORDER_BY(payment_year.ASC(), payment_month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q57] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start and end dates for the 3-month period
# 'current_date' refers to today's date.
# '-3 months' shifts the date back by 3 months.
start_date_period = DATETIME('current_date', '-3 months')
end_date_period = DATETIME('current_date')

top_sales_reps = Salespersons.CALCULATE(
    salesperson_first_name=first_name,
    salesperson_last_name=last_name,
    # Filter the sales made by each salesperson to be within the last 3 months
    filtered_sales_in_period=sales_made.WHERE(
        (sale_date >= start_date_period) & (sale_date <= end_date_period)
    )
).CALCULATE(
    salesperson_first_name=salesperson_first_name,
    salesperson_last_name=salesperson_last_name,
    # Calculate the total number of sales for the filtered period
    total_sales_count=COUNT(filtered_sales_in_period),
    # Calculate the total revenue (sum of sale_price) for the filtered period
    total_revenue=SUM(filtered_sales_in_period.sale_price)
).TOP_K(3, by=total_revenue.DESC()) # Select the top 3 by total revenue in descending order

[DEBUG] [Q60] Running models for: show me the daily total amount of payments received in the whole of the previous ISO week not including the current week, split by the payment_method
[DEBUG] [Q60] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q60] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q60] Code generated by claude:
# Filter payments from the previous ISO week and group by date and payment method
previous_week_payments = PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    # Get payments from 14 days ago to 7 days ago (previous ISO week)
    (DATEDIFF("days", payment_date, DATETIME("now")) >= 7) &
    (DATEDIFF("days", payment_date, DATETIME("now")) < 14) &
    # Ensure we're getting the correct ISO week by checking day of week alignment
    (DAYOFWEEK(payment_date) >= DAYOFWEEK(DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-7 days"))) &
    (DAYOFWEEK(payment_date) <= DAYOFWEEK(DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-1 day")))
).CALCULATE(
    payment_date_only = payment_date,
    payment_method_type = payment_method,
    amount = payment_amount
)

# Group by date and payment method to get daily totals
result = previous_week_payments.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_groups", 
    by=(payment_date_only, payment_method_type)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_date = payment_date_only,
    payment_method = payment_method_type,
    daily_total = SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
).ORDER_BY(payment_date.ASC(), payment_method.ASC())
DATEDIFF unsupported for 'DAYS'.
DATEDIFF unsupported for 'DAYS'.
[DEBUG] [Q60] DataFrame from claude is valid
[DEBUG] [Q60] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Calculate the date range for the previous ISO week
# Get the current date
current_date_val = DATETIME('current_date')

# Calculate the start of the current ISO week (e.g., Monday, depending on config)
start_of_current_iso_week = DATETIME(current_date_val, 'start of week')

# Calculate the start of the previous ISO week (7 days before the current week's start)
start_of_previous_iso_week = DATETIME(start_of_current_iso_week, '-7 days')

# Calculate the end of the previous ISO week (one day before the current week's start)
end_of_previous_iso_week = DATETIME(start_of_current_iso_week, '-1 day')

# Step 2: Filter PaymentsReceived for the calculated date range
# and prepare for partitioning by extracting the date and method
filtered_payments_info = Dealership.PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_previous_iso_week) & (payment_date <= end_of_previous_iso_week)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_day=payment_date,
    method_type=payment_method,
    amount=payment_amount
)

# Step 3: Group by payment_day and method_type, and sum the payment_amount
daily_total_payments_by_method = filtered_payments_info.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_groups",
    by=(payment_day, method_type)
).CALCULATE(
    daily_date=payment_day,
    payment_method_type=method_type,
    # Sum the original payment_amount from the PaymentsReceived collection within each group
    total_amount_received=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Step 4: Select the final fields and order the results
result = daily_total_payments_by_method.CALCULATE(
    daily_date,
    payment_method_type,
    total_amount_received
).ORDER_BY(daily_date.ASC(), payment_method_type.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q61] Running models for: What were the total quarterly sales in 2023 grouped by customer's state? Represent each quarter as the first date in the quarter.
[DEBUG] [Q61] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q61] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q61] Code generated by claude:
# Filter sales for 2023 and add quarter information
sales_2023_info = Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=customer.state,
    quarter=((MONTH(sale_date) - 1) // 3) + 1,
    quarter_start_date=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of year', 
                               JOIN_STRINGS(' ', ((((MONTH(sale_date) - 1) // 3) * 3), 'months')),
    sale_amount=sale_price
)

# Group by state and quarter start date to calculate total sales
result = sales_2023_info.PARTITION(name="state_quarters", by=(customer_state, quarter_start_date)).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=customer_state,
    quarter_start_date=quarter_start_date,
    total_quarterly_sales=SUM(Sales.sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(customer_state.ASC(), quarter_start_date.ASC())
[DEBUG] [Q61] DataFrame from claude is None
[DEBUG] [Q61] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter sales for 2023 and calculate intermediate fields
sales_info = Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=customer.state,
    sale_year=YEAR(sale_date),
    # Determine the start month of the quarter based on the sale_date's month
    quarter_start_month=IFF(MONTH(sale_date) <= 3, 1, IFF(MONTH(sale_date) <= 6, 4, IFF(MONTH(sale_date) <= 9, 7, 10))),
    sale_price=sale_price # Keep sale_price for later aggregation
)

# Step 2: Calculate the quarter's first date using the intermediate fields
# This new field 'quarter_first_date' will be used for partitioning
sales_with_quarter_date = sales_info.CALCULATE(
    customer_state=customer_state, # Pass through customer_state
    quarter_first_date=DATETIME(JOIN_STRINGS('-', sale_year, LPAD(quarter_start_month, 2, '0'), '01')),
    sale_price=sale_price # Pass through sale_price
)

# Step 3: Group by customer state and the calculated quarter's first date, then sum sales price
result = sales_with_quarter_date.PARTITION(
    name="quarterly_state_groups",
    by=(customer_state, quarter_first_date)
).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=customer_state,
    quarter_start_date=quarter_first_date,
    total_quarterly_sales=SUM(Sales.sale_price) # Sum the original sale_price from the Sales collection
).ORDER_BY(customer_state.ASC(), quarter_start_date.ASC()) # Order for consistent output

    total_quarterly_sales=SUM(Sales.sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(customer_state.ASC(), quarter_start_date.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q61] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter sales for 2023 and calculate intermediate fields
sales_info = Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=customer.state,
    sale_year=YEAR(sale_date),
    # Determine the start month of the quarter based on the sale_date's month
    quarter_start_month=IFF(MONTH(sale_date) <= 3, 1, IFF(MONTH(sale_date) <= 6, 4, IFF(MONTH(sale_date) <= 9, 7, 10))),
    sale_price=sale_price # Keep sale_price for later aggregation
)

# Step 2: Calculate the quarter's first date using the intermediate fields
# This new field 'quarter_first_date' will be used for partitioning
sales_with_quarter_date = sales_info.CALCULATE(
    customer_state=customer_state, # Pass through customer_state
    quarter_first_date=DATETIME(JOIN_STRINGS('-', sale_year, LPAD(quarter_start_month, 2, '0'), '01')),
    sale_price=sale_price # Pass through sale_price
)

# Step 3: Group by customer state and the calculated quarter's first date, then sum sales price
result = sales_with_quarter_date.PARTITION(
    name="quarterly_state_groups",
    by=(customer_state, quarter_first_date)
).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=customer_state,
    quarter_start_date=quarter_first_date,
    total_quarterly_sales=SUM(Sales.sale_price) # Sum the original sale_price from the Sales collection
).ORDER_BY(customer_state.ASC(), quarter_start_date.ASC()) # Order for consistent output

# Get treatments from the last 3 months (excluding current month)
treatment_info = Treatments.CALCULATE(
    treatment_month = DATETIME(start_dt, 'start of month'),
    is_biologic = drug.drug_type == 'biologic'
).WHERE(
    # Last 3 months excluding current month
    (DATEDIFF("months", treatment_month, DATETIME('now', 'start of month')) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", treatment_month, DATETIME('now', 'start of month')) <= 3)
)

# Group by month and calculate patient counts

    month = treatment_month,

).ORDER_BY(month.DESC())
[DEBUG] [Q64] DataFrame from claude is valid
[DEBUG] [Q64] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Define the date range for the last 3 months, excluding the current month.
# Get the start of the current month
current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
# Get the start of 3 months ago from the current month's start
three_months_ago_start = DATETIME(current_month_start, '-3 month')

# Step 2: Filter treatments within the desired date range and extract necessary information.
# This includes the month/year for grouping, patient_id, and a flag to identify biologic drugs.
relevant_treatments_info = DermTreatment.Treatments.WHERE(
    # Filter treatments that started within the last 3 full months (e.g., if current month is Oct, include July, Aug, Sep)
    (start_dt >= three_months_ago_start) & (start_dt < current_month_start)
).CALCULATE(
    # Format month as YYYY-MM for consistent grouping and ordering
    treatment_month_year=JOIN_STRINGS('-', TO_STRING(YEAR(start_dt)), LPAD(TO_STRING(MONTH(start_dt)), 2, '0')),
    patient_id=patient_id,
    # Create a flag to identify treatments with biologic drugs (drug_type = 'biologic')
    is_biologic_treatment=IFF(drug.drug_type == 'biologic', 1, 0)
)

# Step 3: Partition the filtered treatments by month to group data for aggregation.
monthly_patient_groups = relevant_treatments_info.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_data", by=treatment_month_year
)

# Step 4: Calculate the distinct patient count and distinct biologic treatment patient count for each month.
monthly_stats = monthly_patient_groups.CALCULATE(
    month=treatment_month_year,
    # Count distinct patients who had any treatment in this month within the partitioned group
    patient_count=NDISTINCT(relevant_treatments_info.patient_id),
    # Count distinct patients who had a biologic treatment in this month within the partitioned group
    biologic_treatment_count=NDISTINCT(relevant_treatments_info.WHERE(is_biologic_treatment == 1).patient_id)
)

# Step 5: Order the results by month in ascending order.
result = monthly_stats.ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# and a flag to identify biologic treatments.

).CALCULATE(

# Calculate the start of the current month
current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Calculate the start of the 6-month period (6 months before the current month's start)
# This effectively gives us the start date for the 7th month prior to the current one,
# ensuring we capture the full 6 months *before* the current one.
six_months_ago_start = DATETIME(current_month_start, '- 6 months')

# Filter for patient Alice and then filter her treatments by the calculated date range
alice_treatments_in_period = Patients.WHERE(
    first_name == "Alice"
).treatments_received.WHERE(
    (start_dt >= six_months_ago_start) & (start_dt < current_month_start)
)

# Count the number of treatments that meet the criteria
result = DermTreatment.CALCULATE(
    num_treatments_for_alice=COUNT(alice_treatments_in_period)
)

[DEBUG] [Q69] Code generated by gemini:
# 1. Calculate the number of distinct drugs prescribed by each doctor.
#    We need doc_id and speciality to carry them forward for the final result and partitioning.
doctor_drug_stats = Doctors.CALCULATE(
    doc_id=doc_id,
    speciality=speciality,
    num_distinct_drugs=NDISTINCT(prescribed_treatments.drug_id)
)

# 2. Partition the doctors by their specialty to enable ranking within each specialty.
#    Then, calculate the SDRSDR (rank) for each doctor based on the number of distinct drugs prescribed,
#    ranking in descending order within their respective specialty group.
result = doctor_drug_stats.PARTITION(name="specialty_groups", by=speciality).CALCULATE(
    doc_id=doc_id,
    speciality=speciality,
    num_distinct_drugs=num_distinct_drugs,
    SDRSDR=RANKING(by=num_distinct_drugs.DESC(), per="specialty_groups")
)

[DEBUG] [Q71] Running models for: What are the PMPD and PMTC for each of the last 12 months, not including the current monthPMPD = per month patient diagnoses. PMTC = per month treatment count. Truncate start_dt to month for aggregation.
[DEBUG] [Q71] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q71] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q71] Code generated by claude:
# Get the last 12 months excluding current month
treatment_info = Treatments.CALCULATE(
    treatment_month = DATETIME(start_dt, 'start of month')
).WHERE(
    # Filter for last 12 months excluding current month
    (DATEDIFF("months", treatment_month, DATETIME('now', 'start of month')) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", treatment_month, DATETIME('now', 'start of month')) <= 12)
)

# Group by month and calculate PMPD and PMTC
result = treatment_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=treatment_month).CALCULATE(
    month = treatment_month,
    PMPD = NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient_id),  # Per Month Patient Diagnoses
    PMTC = COUNT(Treatments)                  # Per Month Treatment Count
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())
[DEBUG] [Q71] DataFrame from claude is valid
[DEBUG] [Q71] Code generated by gemini:
# Calculate the start of the current month and the start of the month 12 months ago
current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
twelve_months_ago_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-12 months')

# Prepare treatment data by truncating start_dt to month and filtering for the last 12 full months
treatment_monthly_info = Treatments.CALCULATE(
    treatment_month=DATETIME(start_dt, 'start of month'),
    patient_id=patient_id,
    diag_id=diag_id
).WHERE(
    (treatment_month >= twelve_months_ago_start) &
    (treatment_month < current_month_start)
)

# Partition the filtered treatments by month and calculate PMPD and PMTC
monthly_metrics = treatment_monthly_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=treatment_month).CALCULATE(
    month_start_date=treatment_month,
    PMTC=COUNT(Treatments),
    PMPD=NDISTINCT(JOIN_STRINGS('-', Treatments.patient_id, Treatments.diag_id))
)

# Order the results by month
result = monthly_metrics.ORDER_BY(month_start_date.ASC())

current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
twelve_months_ago_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-12 months')

# Prepare treatment data by truncating start_dt to month and filtering for the last 12 full months
treatment_monthly_info = Treatments.CALCULATE(
    treatment_month=DATETIME(start_dt, 'start of month'),
    patient_id=patient_id,
    diag_id=diag_id
).WHERE(
    (treatment_month >= twelve_months_ago_start) &
    (treatment_month < current_month_start)
)

# Partition the filtered treatments by month and calculate PMPD and PMTC
monthly_metrics = treatment_monthly_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=treatment_month).CALCULATE(
    month_start_date=treatment_month,
    PMTC=COUNT(Treatments),
    PMPD=NDISTINCT(JOIN_STRINGS('-', Treatments.patient_id, Treatments.diag_id))
)

# Order the results by month
result = monthly_metrics.ORDER_BY(month_start_date.ASC())

# Step 1: Find each patient's first treatment year
patient_first_treatment = Patients.WHERE(
    HAS(treatments_received) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    patient_id=patient_id,
    first_treatment_year=YEAR(MIN(treatments_received.start_dt))
)

    name="years", by=first_treatment_year

    year=year,

)

[DEBUG] [Q74] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: For each patient, find the earliest treatment start date and extract its year.
# We start from the Treatments collection, partition by patient_id to group all treatments for a single patient.
# Then, within each patient's group, we find the minimum start_dt.
# After that, we extract the year from this minimum date.
patient_first_treatment_year_info = Treatments.PARTITION(
    name="patient_treatments", by=patient_id
).CALCULATE(
    patient_id=patient_id,
    first_treatment_date=MIN(Treatments.start_dt)
).CALCULATE(
    patient_id=patient_id,
    first_treatment_year=YEAR(first_treatment_date)
)

# Step 2: Count the number of new patients for each year.
# We partition the `patient_first_treatment_year_info` by the `first_treatment_year`
# to group all patients whose first treatment occurred in that specific year.
# Then, we count the number of unique patient IDs within each year group.
new_patients_per_year_counts = patient_first_treatment_year_info.PARTITION(
    name="yearly_patients", by=first_treatment_year
).CALCULATE(
    year=first_treatment_year,
    num_new_patients=COUNT(patient_id)
)

# Step 3 & 4: Calculate NPI and select the final output.
# We use the PREV window function to get the `num_new_patients` from the previous year.
# `default=0` ensures that for the first year, the previous count is treated as 0,
# so the NPI is simply the number of new patients for that year.
# Finally, we calculate NPI and order the results by year.
result = new_patients_per_year_counts.CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    num_new_patients=num_new_patients,
    prev_num_new_patients=PREV(num_new_patients, by=year.ASC(), default=0)
).CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    num_new_patients=num_new_patients,
    NPI=num_new_patients - prev_num_new_patients
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q77] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Calculate the year and month for both the adverse event reported date
# and the treatment start date for each adverse event.
event_and_treatment_dates = AdverseEvents.CALCULATE(
    drug_id_val=treatment.drug.drug_id,
    drug_name_val=treatment.drug.drug_name,
    event_year=YEAR(reported_dt),
    event_month=MONTH(reported_dt),
    treatment_year=YEAR(treatment.start_dt),
    treatment_month=MONTH(treatment.start_dt)
)

# Step 2: Filter for adverse events where the reported month and year match the treatment start month and year.
filtered_events_same_month = event_and_treatment_dates.WHERE(
    (event_year == treatment_year) & (event_month == treatment_month)
)

# Step 3: Partition the filtered events by drug ID and name, then count the number of adverse events for each drug.
drug_adverse_event_counts = filtered_events_same_month.PARTITION(
    name="drug_groups", by=(drug_id_val, drug_name_val)
).CALCULATE(
    drug_id=drug_id_val,
    drug_name=drug_name_val,
    num_adverse_events=COUNT(AdverseEvents) # Count the original AdverseEvents within each drug group
)

# Step 4: Find the drug with the highest number of adverse events.
result = drug_adverse_event_counts.TOP_K(1, by=num_adverse_events.DESC())

        (MONTH(reported_dt) == MONTH(treatment.start_dt))
    ))
)

[DEBUG] [Q79] Code generated by gemini:
# 1. Filter treatments that ended in the year 2022 and prepare necessary fields.
# We extract patient's insurance type and patient ID into new fields
# so they can be used for partitioning and distinct counting.
treatments_2022_info = Treatments.WHERE(YEAR(end_dt) == 2022).CALCULATE(
    patient_insurance_type=patient.ins_type,
    patient_id_for_distinct_count=patient.patient_id
)

# 2. Partition the filtered treatments by the patient's insurance type.
insurance_type_groups = treatments_2022_info.PARTITION(
    name="insurance_groups",
    by=patient_insurance_type
)

# 3. For each insurance type group, calculate the average day100 PASI score
# and the number of distinct patients.
# 'Treatments' refers to the collection within the 'insurance_groups' partition.
# 'outcome_records' is a plural sub-collection, so AVG is used for day100_pasi_score.
# 'patient_id_for_distinct_count' is a singular field from the partitioned collection,
# so NDISTINCT is used directly on it.
aggregated_results = insurance_type_groups.CALCULATE(
    insurance_type=patient_insurance_type,
    average_pasi_score_day100=AVG(Treatments.outcome_records.day100_pasi_score),
    distinct_patients_count=NDISTINCT(patient_id_for_distinct_count)
)

# 4. Select the top 5 insurance types, sorted by the lowest average PASI score first.
result = aggregated_results.TOP_K(5, by=average_pasi_score_day100.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q86] Running models for: What are the top 3 doctor specialties by total drug amount prescribed for treatments started in the past 6 calendar months? Return the specialty, number of treatments, and total drug amount.
[DEBUG] [Q86] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q86] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q86] Code generated by claude:
# Filter treatments started in the past 6 calendar months
recent_treatments = Treatments.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("months", start_dt, DATETIME('now')) <= 6
)

# Group by doctor specialty and calculate aggregates
specialty_stats = recent_treatments.PARTITION(name="specialties", by=doctor.speciality).CALCULATE(
    specialty=doctor.speciality,
    num_treatments=COUNT(Treatments),
    total_drug_amount=SUM(Treatments.tot_drug_amt)
)

# Get top 3 specialties by total drug amount
result = specialty_stats.TOP_K(3, by=total_drug_amount.DESC())
[DEBUG] [Q86] DataFrame from claude is None
[DEBUG] [Q86] Code generated by gemini:
# Calculate the start date for the "past 6 calendar months"
# This gets the current timestamp, subtracts 6 months, and then truncates to the start of that month.
cutoff_date = DATETIME('now', '-6 months', 'start of month')

# Filter treatments that started within the last 6 calendar months
# and prepare the specialty and drug amount for aggregation.
recent_treatments_info = Treatments.WHERE(
    start_dt >= cutoff_date
).CALCULATE(
    doc_specialty=doctor.speciality,
    treatment_drug_amount=tot_drug_amt
)

# Partition the filtered treatments by doctor specialty
# and calculate the number of treatments and total drug amount for each specialty.
specialty_summary = recent_treatments_info.PARTITION(
    name="specialty_groups", by=doc_specialty
).CALCULATE(
    specialty=doc_specialty,
    num_treatments=COUNT(recent_treatments_info),
    total_drug_amount=SUM(recent_treatments_info.treatment_drug_amount)
)

# Select the top 3 specialties based on the total drug amount prescribed.
result = specialty_summary.TOP_K(
    3, by=total_drug_amount.DESC()
)

    num_treatments=COUNT(Drugs.treatments_used_in),
    avg_drug_amount=AVG(Drugs.treatments_used_in.tot_drug_amt)
).TOP_K(5, by=num_treatments.DESC()).CALCULATE(
    drug_name=drug_name,
    num_treatments=num_treatments,
    avg_drug_amount=avg_drug_amount
)

[DEBUG] [Q89] Code generated by gemini:
# Filter treatments based on the specified criteria
filtered_treatments = DermTreatment.Treatments.WHERE(
    # Condition 1: Diagnosis name contains 'psoriasis' (case-insensitive)
    (LOWER(diagnosis.diag_name) LIKE '%psoriasis%')
    &
    # Condition 2: The drug involved has an FDA approval date (is not null)
    (drug.fda_appr_dt != None)
    &
    # Condition 3: The treatment ended within the last 6 months from today
    # DATEDIFF("months", start_date, end_date) returns the number of full months between dates.
    # So, <= 6 means it ended within the current month up to 6 full months ago.
    (DATEDIFF("months", end_dt, DATETIME('now')) <= 6)
)

# Count the number of treatments that meet all criteria
result = DermTreatment.CALCULATE(
    num_matching_treatments=COUNT(filtered_treatments)
)

# Calculate the target registration year (2 years ago from the current date)
target_registration_year = YEAR(DATETIME('current_date')) - 2

# Filter doctors who were registered exactly 2 years ago
# Then, for each of these doctors, find their first treatment and extract the required details
result = Doctors.WHERE(
    year_reg == target_registration_year
).CALCULATE(
    # Select the doctor's last name and year of registration
    doctor_last_name=last_name,
    doctor_registration_year=year_reg,
    # Find the first treatment for each doctor by ranking their prescribed treatments
    # based on the start date in ascending order (earliest first).
    # 'per="Doctors"' ensures the ranking is performed independently for each doctor.
    # '.SINGULAR()' is used because RANKING(by=...)=1 guarantees a single record (or ties) per doctor.
    first_treatment_record=prescribed_treatments.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=start_dt.ASC(), per="Doctors") == 1
    ).SINGULAR()
).CALCULATE(
    # Project the final desired fields from the doctor and the identified first treatment
    last_name=doctor_last_name,
    year_of_registration=doctor_registration_year,
    first_treatment_date=first_treatment_record.start_dt,
    first_treatment_id=first_treatment_record.treatment_id
)

).CALCULATE(

[DEBUG] [Q94] Running models for: Calculate the CPUR for each merchant, considering only successful transactions. Return the merchant name and CPUR.CPUR (coupon usage rate) = number of distinct coupons used / number of distinct transactions
[DEBUG] [Q94] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q94] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q94] Code generated by gemini:
merchants_with_cpur = Merchants.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=name,
    # Calculate the number of distinct coupons used in successful transactions for each merchant.
    # NDISTINCT automatically handles non-null values.
    num_distinct_coupons=NDISTINCT(transactions_received.WHERE(status == "successful").coupon_id),
    # Calculate the total number of distinct successful transactions for each merchant.
    num_distinct_transactions=COUNT(transactions_received.WHERE(status == "successful").txid)
).CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    # Calculate CPUR (Coupon Usage Rate).
    # Use IFF to handle cases where there are no successful transactions to avoid division by zero.
    cpur=IFF(num_distinct_transactions > 0,
             num_distinct_coupons * 1.0 / num_distinct_transactions,
             0.0)
)
[DEBUG] [Q94] DataFrame from gemini is valid
[DEBUG] [Q94] Code generated by claude:
# Get all successful transactions for each merchant (both sent and received)
merchant_transaction_data = Merchants.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=name,
    successful_transactions_sent=transactions_sent.WHERE(status == "successful"),
    successful_transactions_received=transactions_received.WHERE(status == "successful")
).CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    # Combine all successful transactions (sent + received)
    total_successful_transactions=COUNT(successful_transactions_sent) + COUNT(successful_transactions_received),
    # Count distinct coupons used in successful transactions
    distinct_coupons_used=NDISTINCT(successful_transactions_sent.coupon_id) + NDISTINCT(successful_transactions_received.coupon_id)
).WHERE(total_successful_transactions > 0).CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    cpur=distinct_coupons_used / total_successful_transactions
)

# Get all successful transactions for each merchant (both sent and received)
merchant_transaction_data = Merchants.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=name,
    # Count distinct successful transactions (both sent and received)
    total_successful_transactions=NDISTINCT(
        transactions_sent.WHERE(status == "SUCCESS").txid
    ) + NDISTINCT(
        transactions_received.WHERE(status == "SUCCESS").txid
    ),
    # Count distinct coupons used in successful transactions (both sent and received)
    distinct_coupons_used=NDISTINCT(
        transactions_sent.WHERE((status == "SUCCESS") & (coupon_id != None)).coupon_id
    ) + NDISTINCT(
        transactions_received.WHERE((status == "SUCCESS") & (coupon_id != None)).coupon_id
    )
).WHERE(total_successful_transactions > 0)

# Calculate CPUR for each merchant
result = merchant_transaction_data.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    cpur=distinct_coupons_used / total_successful_transactions
)

    merchant_name=name,
    successful_transactions_sent=transactions_sent.WHERE(status == "completed"),
    successful_transactions_received=transactions_received.WHERE(status == "completed")
).CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    all_successful_transactions=successful_transactions_sent + successful_transactions_received
).CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    distinct_coupons_used=NDISTINCT(all_successful_transactions.WHERE(coupon_id != None).coupon_id),
    total_distinct_transactions=NDISTINCT(all_successful_transactions.txid),
    cpur=IFF(
        NDISTINCT(all_successful_transactions.txid) > 0,
        NDISTINCT(all_successful_transactions.WHERE(coupon_id != None).coupon_id) / NDISTINCT(all_successful_transactions.txid),
        0
    )
)

    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    cpur=cpur

[DEBUG] [Q95] Running models for: For users in the US and Canada, how many total notifications were sent in each of the last 3 weeks excluding the current week? How many of those were sent on weekends?Weekends are Saturdays and Sundays. Truncate created_at to week for aggregation.
[DEBUG] [Q95] Running model claude (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q95] Running model gemini (attempt 0)
[DEBUG] [Q95] Code generated by claude:
# Filter notifications for US and Canada users from last 3 weeks (excluding current week)
us_canada_notifications = Users.WHERE(
    ISIN(country, ("US", "Canada"))
).notifications.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("weeks", created_at, DATETIME("now")) >= 1 &
    DATEDIFF("weeks", created_at, DATETIME("now")) <= 3
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(created_at, "start of week"),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(created_at), (1, 7))  # Saturday=7, Sunday=1
)

# Group by week and calculate metrics
result = us_canada_notifications.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start=week_start,
    total_notifications=COUNT(Notifications),
    weekend_notifications=SUM(Notifications.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())
[DEBUG] [Q95] DataFrame from claude is None
[DEBUG] [Q95] Code generated by gemini:
# Calculate the start of the current week and the three preceding weeks at the Ewallet (graph) level
# This makes these calculated timestamps available for filtering downstream.
ewallet_time_context = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start=DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    week_1_ago=DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-1 week'),
    week_2_ago=DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-2 weeks'),
    week_3_ago=DATETIME(DATETIME('now', 'start of week'), '-3 weeks')
)

# Filter users from US or Canada, then access their notifications.
# For each notification, calculate its week (truncated to start of week) and if it was sent on a weekend.
notifications_with_week_info = ewallet_time_context.Users.WHERE(
    ISIN(country, ("US", "Canada"))
).notifications.CALCULATE(
    notification_week=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(created_at), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
)

# Filter these notifications to include only those from the last 3 weeks, excluding the current week.
# The 'week_X_ago' variables are accessible from the 'ewallet_time_context'.
filtered_notifications_for_past_weeks = notifications_with_week_info.WHERE(
    (notification_week == week_1_ago) |
    (notification_week == week_2_ago) |
    (notification_week == week_3_ago)
)

# Partition the filtered notifications by their respective week.
# Then, for each week, calculate the total number of notifications and the number of notifications sent on weekends.
result = filtered_notifications_for_past_weeks.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_notifications_group", by=notification_week
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=notification_week,
    total_notifications_sent=COUNT(Notifications),
    weekend_notifications_sent=SUM(Notifications.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start_date.DESC()) # Order the results by week, from most recent to oldest
